# Relay compositor — programme index

The entry point for any session working on this repository. Read this file first, then
`docs/_RULES.md` (written alongside the R2 plan).

| 2026-10-15 | **C00 gate PASS** (items 1, 2-partial, 3, 5; `_FINDINGS.md` F-001..F-005, spike `spike/t00` @738eb88, never merged). 720p30 full chain 0.83 of 4 cores; SEI survives (ADR 0006 takes the "survives" branch). **`_FINDINGS.md` overrides any brief it contradicts.** Owed to the owner session: delay median, phone reconnect, YouTube go-live + gap tolerance, Poor-health cause with real footage. | F-001..F-005 |
| 2026-10-15 | **C00 owner session (real capture app, stg fixture 5):** delay in-chain ~3.1 s (n=2; separate reader reads ~300 ms high), provisional `overlayDelayMs` default 3100, n≥10 in-chain sample owed before C11 fixes it. App backgrounding: no gap seen (owner timing unconfirmed). YouTube "Poor" is NOT filler (0 % filler, flat 4.16 Mbps, recovered by itself); suspect CF Output→YouTube leg, direct-to-YouTube probe owed. YouTube kept the SAME broadcast across 5 s and 15 s gaps, but a **30 s gap started a NEW broadcast** (owner-confirmed); after the last frame it auto-ended ~60–90 s later. So the split threshold is **between 15 s and 30 s**: every R2 recovery path that the organiser's broadcast must survive has to finish **under 15 s**, measured as output-gap. That covers output reconnect, in-place restart, and CEF restart. A new-attempt cold start (~25 s boot + warm) will likely SPLIT the broadcast; the runbook must say so, and ADR 0004/C14 budgets are to be re-checked against 15 s. One single-shot RTMPS restart failed ("Socket I/O timed out"), so output reconnect must retry (C04/C09). | F-002, F-004 |

| 2026-10-15 | **C04, correcting its brief (F-005):** `OutputLink` reads bytes SENT, never acks. `on_progress(now, gen, total_bytes)` takes the sink's own monotonic total, counted from 0 for each new sink (C09: rtmp2sink `out-bytes-total`). Connected is the first total above 0 after a build. Stalled is no growth for `output_stall_after_ms` (renamed from `output_ack_stall_ms`; still 5 s), and the stall swaps the sink at once. A total that repeats or goes backwards is no progress. `on_connected` and `on_ack` are gone, so C06's `OutputConnected`/`OutputAck` become one `OutputProgress { gen, total_bytes }` (Owed 13). A connect that times out, rtmp2sink's "Socket I/O timed out" included, re-enters the 0.5 s → 2 s back-off and never gives up. `OUTPUT_GAP_BUDGET_MS` = 15 000 joins `config.rs` as the F-004 split budget. | — (C04; C06, C09, C14 owe) |

| 2026-10-16 | **Coordinator decision I4 supersedes ADR 0007 host-contract item 4, in part.** Item 4 said SIGINT and SIGTERM "are handled the same". Under I4 they're not: SIGINT is web's own stop (hold the Final scene, then drain), and SIGTERM is a stop the host starts (drain at once, no Final card). A host driver must name SIGINT for web's stop and leave SIGTERM to the host. The rest of item 4 stands: accept either signal and wait before SIGKILL. The wait is now the 20 s grace (D15), since our stop holds the Final scene first. F-008 measured this on Fly. ADR 0007 carries a dated note, and its original text is unchanged. | 0007 (superseded in part); F-008 |
| 2026-10-16 | **C05, as built:** `Graphics` and `Ladder` keep every name and signature the brief declared, so C06 consumes them unchanged. Failed is **recoverable**, as the brief says (a flaky page deploy early in a match must not cost the score bug for the whole match): `cef_fail_window_ms` after the hide, the page restarts. By then every failure that led to Failed is at least the window old (the wording of `graphics.rs`'s Failed bullet), so the retried page gets its full `cef_fail_count` attempts again, and a page that never recovers hides once per cooldown. At exactly `retry_at` the failure that entered Failed is exactly the window old and still counts (`Window` keeps a stamp exactly `span` old), so a crash on that very tick backs off as the second failure (`Backoff { attempt: 1 }`, 2 s), not the first. The page still gets its full attempts before the next Failed; only that one back-off doubles. The brief's one-tick form never hits this, and C11's 1 s pid poll makes it unreachable in practice; the generated sweep pins it. The cooldown's end is the restart itself, on the same tick; the brief went through a one-tick `Backoff` first. A crash reported while backing off or Failed is not counted twice. A ready counts only from `Starting` of the current generation, so a late, stale or second ready changes nothing and cannot push the recycle out. Every deadline saturates. The ladder never steps back up (brief: YAGNI until the soak shows a need); with C09's `FastPreset` that costs the score bug for the rest of the match (Owed 20). `FAILURE_HISTORY_CAP` (32) and `OVERRUN_HISTORY_CAP` (64) are public, and each is pinned at or above its count. | — (C05) |
| 2026-10-16 | **Coordinator (C05 review), on Owed 17:** C11 never sets the overlay slot from a generation until that generation's own `seazn:ready` line, and C08 maps `HideOverlay` to `programme.hide_overlay()` **and** `graphics.stop()`. Both are tested in both directions: nothing of the hidden generation reaches air, and the retried generation's frames do once it signals ready. C05's same-tick restart at `retry_at` is kept: the review measured restart rates identical to the brief's one-tick form (36 an hour, at least 1 056 ms apart, at most 3 in any 10 s, on the 33 ms tick). Owed 18 (hang detection) and Owed 20 (the ladder's bottom rung) go to the owner. | — (Owed 17, 18, 20) |
| 2026-10-16 | **C11 Step 0, outcome (a) (F-009): loopback is the primary phase channel, with C12's headers and no CEF flags. Caveat (review I1): the probe page had no CSP, and the real overlay page's `connect-src` omits the endpoint (Owed 23).** A public HTTPS page (a `trycloudflare.com` quick tunnel) in the F-001 image (CEF 139.0.28, Fly lhr, one fresh Machine per run) fetched `http://127.0.0.1:7419/phase?after=0`. V2 (`--variant pna`, no flags) answered `seazn:probe=ok:{"phase":"live","seq":1}`, V3a and V3b (one `disable-features` each) answered the same, and V4 held 9 s and came back intact. V1 (no CORS headers) was blocked by plain CORS. CEF 139 sends the PNA preflight but does not enforce its result: the GET followed a preflight that came back with no CORS header. So what lets the page through today is the GET's exact-origin `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. The observed `Origin` form is `https://<host>`, with no default port and no trailing slash. R2005's hook starts in `loopback` (cite F-009). **Coordinator decision on the review (2026-10-16): R2005 owns the CSP.** It adds `http://127.0.0.1:${RELAY_PHASE_PORT}` to `connect-src` for the `/overlay` tree only (change 8), and its fetch stays CORS-simple (change 9). C11 Step 3 sets no `chrome-extra-flags`. C12 ships its headers as briefed: ACAPN is kept because it is harmless and the brief asks for it, not as future-proofing, since Chromium replaced PNA enforcement with the Local Network Access permission. C12's `allowed_origin` must equal the composed `overlayUrl`'s origin, and stg has two hosts (C12 amendment). The compatibility step (G1) did not run, because R2005 is not on stg (Owed 21). A CEF bump past Chrome 142 is expected to land in outcome (c) unless the LNA permission is granted; the G1 step's "≥ 3 `GET /phase`" assertion is the per-release detector (Owed 22). | — (R2005, C11, C12; F-009) |
| 2026-10-18 | **Owner ruling (Owed 20), in chat, relayed by the coordinator: "no lite mode; if CPU impacts, measure and raise the CPU size".** The owner approved three points. **(1)** C15 measures the REAL overlay cost on the planned guest (performance-4x), at 720p and 1080p, on the worst-case encode (the snow background). The page is the production overlay page with real templates: the cricket bar with frequent moments, plus the corner bug. **Size rule:** p95 Machine CPU above 50 % of cores ⇒ move to the next performance size and re-measure. F-005 measured only a test page (0.83 of 4 cores), and this closes that gap. **(2)** The score is never removed. `FastPreset`, whose only possible meaning was "drop the overlay entirely" (F-001; C09's decision rule), is deleted. The ladder is `Full → OverlayHalfRate`, the bottom holds, and frames still drop before the encoder first. R2002's `fast_preset` is removed too (`seazn.club` #980; the contract sha is in Owed 24). **(3)** Any production step-down is a sizing alarm: web raises it from the heartbeat, and C17's runbook says to raise the Machine size (Owed 25). Built with it: `ladder_step_min_gap_ms` is removed, because with one step it gated nothing, and a mutant on it was equivalent | — (C05 code; C02, C06, C07, C09, C14, C15, C17 briefs; `seazn.club` #980) |

| 2026-10-18 | **PR #21 review fixes (docs).** **I1:** a hang hides the overlay at once. Stale means no successful live-data read for `SCORE_STALE_AFTER_MS` = 10 000 (equal to `cef_hang_after_ms`). The compositor host polls at `STREAM_POLL_MS` in every tier (E0: stale within 10 s plus one poll of the feed being cut, fresh on the next success). **(3) Phase channel:** it starts `loopback`. Before the first adopted reply the seq is −1, so the first ask is `after=0`. A failed ask waits `PHASE_RETRY_AFTER_FAILURE_MS` = 1 000 before the next; a success is followed at once. A reply later than the hold plus `PHASE_REPLY_GRACE_MS` = 2 000 is a failure. 3 consecutive failures switch to `web` for the page's life, logged `seazn:phase-channel=web`. A reply whose seq is not newer resets the failure count and leaves the phase. **(4) Alive:** `seazn:alive` every `OVERLAY_ALIVE_EVERY_MS` = 2 000 after ready, from a hook mounted above the scene subtree; E9 sweeps live, reconnecting and final for gaps. **(5) The door:** `host=compositor` is honoured only behind a verified relay access (`sid` + `st`, a `relay-page` token for that sid, a composed session on this fixture, `!isTerminal`). Without both parameters the markup is byte-identical to the public page. A refusal 404s the page the way a missing fixture does; the overlay ROUTE falls through to the public answer instead. A missing `AUTH_SECRET` throws rather than 404ing. The relay PAGE FACTS are read uncached (the access check, and the fixture row off the base tables); the FOLD is cached, under its own namespace `overlay-fold-relay-v4` (keyed on `last_seq`, `revalidate: 300`), never shared with the public read's `overlay-fold-v4` (corrected 2026-10-19, PR #982 review round 1 item 7: this row first said "the relay read is uncached"). **(6) Fallback B:** the route gives a verified request the stored beat's `programmePhase`, `private, no-store`; a beat without one gives none, never a default. | — (R2005; C11 reads (1)–(4)) |

The probe and `the_compositor_runs_non_dumpable_with_a_one_byte_core_limit` now stat `/proc/<pid>/status`, each against a dumpable control: the entrypoint's bash in the image, the test's own `/proc/self/status` in the binary test. (b) Docker's default capability set lacks CAP_SYS_PTRACE, so `docker exec -u 0` cannot read the environment of a process with another uid (all 8 reads failed). The probe reads environments with `docker exec --privileged -u 0` (the exec only, never the container), and an unreadable environment is its own failure. If that holds, what GHCR buys is isolation from gha's slow windows (if GHCR does not share them), plus the 43.7 s gha export a warm run skips. **The fair batch (Owed 78, closed 2026-10-23):** a fill dispatch, then three `measure: ghcr` and three `measure: gha` timing dispatches at one head, side by side. Each waits for the fill to land, so all six build in one window. Each puts a file in the build context, so rust-build's `COPY . .` and everything after it rebuild as on a new commit (the context has no `target/`, so cargo builds from scratch either way). In this window gha served the blob at 10 to 17.5 MB/s and GHCR at 128 to 194 MB/s; the earlier window's 29 to 46 MB/s gha and 73.8 MB/s GHCR show both move with the window, and GHCR led in both. **Timing runs end after the two build steps, and a fill dispatch runs the `cache` job alone** (owner, 2026-10-23, in chat, relayed by the coordinator: "cut measure runs after build steps"). Every later step of the `image` job carries `if: env.TIMING != 'true'`. `scripts/cef_cache_key_test.py` evaluates those guards: on a push or a PR all 17 steps run, a timing run gets 8 and a fill gets 0; 5 guard mutants, all killed. It evaluates the `cache` job's guard too, which holds the write token, over the same 5 events (confirm pass c-2): a push and a fill run it, a PR and both timing runs skip it. The guard is written without parentheses (`github.event_name == 'push' || inputs.measure == 'fill'`; `inputs` is empty on a push and a PR) so the test can evaluate it. 4 mutants, all killed: `always()` (the confirm pass's survivor), the guard deleted, PRs let in, any dispatch let in. **Built:** a new `cache` job (push to main and a fill dispatch only; `packages: write`; checkout without persisted credentials; its actions pinned by commit, m7) builds the runtime target with `outputs: type=cacheonly` and writes `cache-to: type=registry,ref=ghcr.io/<owner>/seazn-relay-cache:cef-<key>,mode=max`. It then refuses a cache with no layer of 1 GB or more (`scripts/cef-cache-holds-tree.sh`). The `image` job (`packages: read`) logs in and probes the ref, and calls it warm only when the manifest holds the tree, through the same script (m3). Warm, it reads GHCR alone. Cold (no key, no tag, an unreadable one, one without the tree), it reads the ref and `type=gha` and writes `type=gha` as before, so it falls back and never fails. The two caches are never read together on a warm run: BuildKit merges its importers' records concurrently and the first to answer serves a shared layer (`solver/combinedcache.go`, `Records`). **Trust (I-1):** any repository writer can write the tag main reads. A dispatch runs the dispatched ref's copy of image.yml with `packages: write`, and a same-repository PR's own copy can ask for it, so any manifest can land at `cef-<key>`, and BuildKit serves its layers as `CACHED` without rebuilding them. That is acceptable for CI, which ships nothing; the release and deploy builds must never `cache-from` `ghcr.io/onryde/seazn-relay-cache` (C16 brief, amendment 2026-10-23, with a `check-workflows.sh` grep and mutant 5). It refuses a stage order other than gst-base, `FROM gst-base AS cef-build`, rust-build; a COPY, ADD, RUN --mount or other ARG in those stages, or a FROM naming a variable (inputs the key cannot see); and a base that does not resolve. In the `image` job a key failure is a cold cache; in the `cache` job, a failure. **Tests:** `scripts/cef_cache_key_test.py` (check.yml's "GHCR cache scripts"), with a fake resolver. It inserts a line after each Dockerfile line (31 inside, a new key; 61 outside, the same key) and appends ` # x` to each non-FROM line (29 inside, 57 outside; this covers the range's last line, which an insert cannot). It also checks each of the 5 FROM lines' bases, a moved digest, a pinned base, 3 commit files, 22 refusals and 9 cache manifests for the tree check, and that the `cache` job's build has the same context, file, build args and target as the two readers (m4). `scripts/cache_timing.py --self-test`, 6 cases. Hand mutants: 30 applied, 29 killed and 1 equivalent. 28 were killed at once. n08 (the resolver's exit status ignored) survived until the case "a resolver that prints a digest and fails" was added, and is now killed. t03 (the tree check's "no layers" refusal) is equivalent: an empty list's largest layer is 0, which fails the floor, so that check is deleted (the review's 11, among them its 3 survivors: `rust - 2`, now red on the edit at line 43; `${rust}`, red on the rust-build FROM case; no `gst < cef` check, red on "cef-build before gst-base"). BuildKit's own per-instruction keys are what keep a stale CEF out; the tag only separates CEF generations. **What is pushed:** cache manifests and the layers of the runtime target's stages only, never cef-test's own and never a runnable relay image; image.yml's header and C12 Step 8 say so (m8). **Retention (m6):** each write leaves the previous manifest as an untagged version of the package, which is private. Blobs dedupe, but each new key (a pin bump, a moved base) keeps about 1.86 GB referenced. Not pruned: whether GHCR lists a BuildKit cache index's blobs as child versions is unverified, and a wrong prune empties the warm cache. Whether GHCR bills private storage here was not checked; making the package public, as the repository is, is the owner's call. **Not chosen, a prebuilt cef-build image:** it needs `build-contexts` or a Dockerfile change, and its own presence probe to fall back, and it still pulls the same 1.86 GB. A Release-only image would pull about 144 MB, but that changes the Dockerfile's stages (out of scope; the next lever if GHCR is not clearly faster). **check.yml's apt hang (run 39931988005): left as is.** The registry approach there needs a CI container image with GStreamer and Rust, plus a publish path, and the tests would then no longer run on the host runner that the supervision test pins (Ubuntu 24.04's bash). Not cheap. **After:** Owed 78 closed; PR #47 merges. | — (CI) |

| 2026-10-23 | **C13 CI round 2 (PR #48 at `8e27239`; mutant run 39984287910: m1's baseline red at `tests/strip.rs:37`, frame 1020 read `None` for counter 3406380220; the review of `dd589dd`, 1 critical, 2 important, 8 minor)** (the coordinator's message of 2026-10-23). The implementer's choices (none is an owner ruling): **(1) The strip on snow.** Frame 1020 is the first keyframe of the snow third (the round trip's frames 1 000–1 099 are snow, with a keyframe every 30). Reproduced on the host with ffmpeg's libx264 at the camera's and the compositor's own settings (veryfast, zerolatency, 4 000 kbps, a keyframe every 60, VBV 1 000 ms, CBR with filler): per-pixel noise starves an I-frame under sliced threads (`sliced-threads=1:threads=4`), and the bottom rows, the strip's, are crushed toward grey. A black guard band alone did not help, and nor did 8-pixel blocks (3 and 7 wrong strips at 4 000 kbps). The camera's snow is now one noise value per 16×16 block (the macroblock grid) above a one-cell black guard over the strip. Measured with ffmpeg through both generations (camera, then the compositor's settings): 0 of 1 130 wrong at 4 000 kbps with 4 and 8 threads, and 0 at 2 000 kbps with 4 threads. At 2 000 kbps with 8 threads, 3 were wrong in the first generation. The assertion is unchanged; on a failure it now prints the frame's kind (ball, snow or slate), the 48 bits wanted and read, and the cells' means. A new media test sends the camera's own snow through its pipeline and the checker's decode (`the_cameras_snow_keeps_its_strip_through_the_checkers_decode`). **(2) Segment dating (I-1).** MediaMTX names a segment by the arrival of its first sample, and each segment's PTS restarts near 0 (F-021, measured). So the first segment after a connect is named before its backlog drained, and read as name plus PTS it runs past the next segment's name (2.0 s on the green smoke). A boundary is judged before any frame is dated. When the earlier segment's last frame (by name plus PTS) would land after the next segment's first, the earlier segment is re-dated backwards from the next one: its end is the next segment's first frame, less the content gap, less one period. The content gap is the camera time between the strips on either side of the boundary, less the programme time the frames between them cover. The segment's frames are never dated before its own name. The overlap is never dropped. It is reported in `segment_overlaps` (its size and its `content_gap_ms`), and a content gap over the outage threshold is an outage, so an outage hidden under a backlog is still reported. `duration_ms` is now the sum of the segments' PTS spans plus one period, and `fps_mean` is frames over that. **(3) A/V offset (I-2).** The checker's decode reads one AAC generation late. Measured on the 0-offset fixture in check run 39979756301: +33 ms, about 1 600 samples, which is voaacenc's encoder delay. `CAMERA_AAC_DELAY_MS` (1 600 / 48 ms) is subtracted from every pair. The fixture is now asserted at 0 ± 5 ms, and a fixture with a known 1 200-sample delay at 25 ± 5 ms. The camera's own TS path must read late by the same constant ± 5 ms (`tests/strip.rs`), which checks the constant independently of the fixture. **(4) The minors.** m1: run-case.sh keeps its console in `out/<case>/console.log`. Its EXIT trap scans on every exit, early ones too, over a flushed console, and writes `secrets.json` (`scanned`, `leaks`). harness.yml's "Leak gate" step uploads `out/` only when that file exists, `scanned > 0` and `leaks == 0`. m2: smoke's `heartbeat_fields` requires `rung`, and the checker fails a run whose beats carry none. m3: fake-control's self-check is `harness::control::self_check`, and a media test reaches its refusals (a session whose `overlayUrl` lost `delay=`, and an id that is not SESSION_ID). m4: a resume's covering span must start within 10 s of the camera's return, else `None`. m5: the frame period is each segment's (last − first)/(n − 1), median over segments (33.333, not the RTMP millisecond step 33.0). m6: done in round 1. m7: cost only, not changed (the `rust-build` stage's `COPY . .`). m8: F-021 and the session handoff are updated. From the Gap Hunt: an output that connects late or dies before the end request is an outage of kind `head` or `tail` (bounded by the first live beat and the end request; outages between segments are `between`). The score strip draws index + 1, so an all-black strip (a slate, a hidden overlay) reads `None`, not index 0 (`score::MAX_INDEX` is 65 534). A freeze needs at least 3 repeats (`FREEZE_MIN_REPEATS`). Shorter repeats are cadence, reported in the new `camera.cadence` (`pairs`, `repeated_frames`, `skipped_counters`, `skip_events`; Owed 81). A C14 verdict over `resumes` owes `length >= 1`. Smoke now has 17 checks: `heartbeat_fields` gains `rung`, and `overlaps_judged_by_content` is new (every overlap carries its content gap). |

| 2026-10-23 | **CI: apt bounded on a trickling mirror** (the coordinator's dispatch, 2026-10-23; branch `ci/apt-retry`). **Before (measured):** check.yml's GStreamer step (`apt-get update && apt-get install`, 107 MB of packages) took 32 to 52 s on five normal runs that day (39980314669, 39977135072, 39966106773, 39951660725, 39936996163: the index update 4 to 10 s, the fetch 5 to 14 s at 7.9 to 22.6 MB/s, the install after it 20 to 34 s), and 1336 to 1512 s on four runs: 39931988005 (1512 s), 39974770999 (1504 s), 39982478989 attempt 1 (main, 1504 s), and 39984134354 attempt 1 (1504 s) and attempt 2 (1336 s). The job's 25 minutes cut all but the last inside the step; the last finished it ("Fetched 107 MB in 21min 52s (81.7 kB/s)") and hit the 25 minutes before cargo test, so every one was cancelled, none failed. **Where it sat (the step logs of 39984134354 attempt 2, 39982478989 attempt 1, 39974770999 and 39931988005):** not the index update (12.5 MB in 1 s from the same host every time) but the package fetch: every file from `azure.archive.ubuntu.com`, the first of the runner's three mirrors (`/etc/apt/apt-mirrors.txt`, 144 B: azure, then archive.ubuntu.com, then security.ubuntu.com), at about 40 to 80 kB/s, gaps of up to 315 s between files (after the 13.6 MB libflite1), and a few `Ign:` lines (fetches that failed part way); no other host. So one mirror trickling, not a dead one: apt moves to the list's next mirror only on an error, and its Timeout bounds only silence. This narrows the false-premise row on run 39931988005: on the apt side it was one mirror's package downloads, not the runner's network as a whole. Main's rerun of 39982478989 (attempt 2, 19:02) took 68 s. **Built:** `scripts/apt-install.sh <packages>`, called by check.yml's GStreamer step with the same package list. The index update (2 tries of 45 s) and a `--download-only` fetch (3 tries of 120 s; each bound at least 4x the slowest normal) each run under `timeout -k 5`, with a `::warning::` for each failed try and one `::error::` naming the last exit code. After a failed try, azure is dropped from the mirror list (never emptied), so the next try fetches from archive.ubuntu.com. The install then runs with `--no-download` and the fetch's own arguments, so it never goes back to the network. Worst case 490 s, inside the step's new `timeout-minutes: 10`, with 110 s left for the install. **apt's own settings are the runner image's (round 2, review I1):** `-o Acquire::Retries=1 -o Acquire::http::Timeout=15 -o Acquire::https::Timeout=15`, the image's `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/zz-retries` (runner-images `configure-apt.sh`, tag `ubuntu24/20261012.334`). Round 1's `Retries=5`, `Timeout=20` were LOOSER, not shorter. Blackhole run 40012067071 showed them: the first `Ign:` came at +20 s, then the same URI was retried at +1, +2, +4 and +8 s, so apt would leave a dead mirror at about 51 s, past the 45 s update bound. With the image's values that is one timeout plus a 1 s retry. The script logs `apt-config dump` of the three settings at the start. `scripts/apt_install_test.py` (check.yml's "apt install script" step) runs the script under a fake sudo, timeout, apt-get, apt-config, tee and sleep; the fake apt-get and the fake tee on the mirror list refuse unless run under sudo. It has 9 cases and makes 66 checks, and the bound is read from check.yml. Its settings checks: no looser than the image's, and (R + 1) x T + (2^R - 1) under the update bound. Mutants (round 2): 35, of which 34 were killed, each by a named check; the reviewer's 7 are among them. One survived: the download tries cut from 3 to 2, which stays bounded and retries once, so it is not a defect. **Proof:** a stall cannot be produced on demand. The PR's check run proves the normal path (round 1: the GStreamer step took 42 s, run 40012050334). Blackhole dispatch 40012067071 (every mirror dead) failed at 95 s with its warnings and error, and it shows the lever working on 24.04: "dropped azure.archive.ubuntu.com", then `Mirrorlist [93 B]`, then `https://archive.ubuntu.com … InRelease`. Round 2's `apt_blackhole` is a choice (dispatch only), and a verdict step checks each mode: `all` must fail under 510 s; `azure` (the first mirror dead) must pass from the next one. A broken fallback fails the `azure` run, and `azure` also measures the fetch from archive.ubuntu.com. Round 2's dispatch runs: `all` 40015866375 failed at 193 s on the script's own `::error::` (its verdict passed), and `azure` 40015870778 passed the step in 127 s (its verdict passed). **Confirm pass (APPROVED at `9fedbc3`, n1 and n2 fixed):** both dispatch steps match `all` and `azure` by name, in the `if:` and in the body, with an `::error::` for any other value. On push and pull_request the input is empty, and round 2's `!= 'none'` left `github.event_name` as the only guard; without it, an empty mode fell into the azure branch. The script writes `<phase> <exit>` to `$APT_INSTALL_RESULT` when it gives up, and `all`'s verdict requires that file, so a crash before the bound does not pass. The test evaluates both `if:` expressions in 7 contexts and runs both bodies against a fixture: 108 checks. Of the 12 new mutants, 11 were killed; the 12th drops the event_name clause, which is equivalent because the by-name match alone refuses an empty input. **Recorded, not changed (n3):** `apt-get update` exits 0 when every mirror fails fast ("W: Some index files failed to download"), so the update loop catches only a wall-clock stall; the fetch then fails on stale lists and the script gives up as it should. Main behaves the same. `--error-on=any` would also fail on a flaky third-party list (packages.microsoft.com). **Not proven:** whether archive.ubuntu.com is fast while azure trickles; the `azure` run measures it only on a normal day. The Dockerfile's apt lists are untouched. | — |
| 2026-10-23 | **Zygote watch at ready (PR #51, branch `fix/zygote-poll-at-ready`): a ready lists CEF's children before it is relayed** (the coordinator's decision 2026-10-23 on the diagnosis of image run 40015873298, medium, under the owner's standing medium delegation; F-022). **The defect (measured, F-022):** 1 of 44 probe zygote kills (exit 6 of 32 in run 40015873298; 1 of the 11 runs that completed the 32-exit sweep since 2026-10-20) got no verdict and no exit 75: `zygote_check` only knows the zygotes a child poll has listed, and the poll starts at gen 1's restart, before CEF forks one, then lists every `CHILD_POLL_MS` (1 s), so the zygotes were first listed by the 2nd poll, at about restart + 1.01 s, while `cef ready gen=1` came at about restart + 0.72 s. The 43 passing kills got the verdict at restart + 2.017 to 2.036 s (the 3rd poll); the failing run's probe was the fastest measured (its ready-to-stop gaps were the 4 shortest of 128, 0.100 to 0.157 s, the median 0.188 s). In production such a death is Chromium's abort (133) at the next GPU-process death, or `CefCrashed` at once on the next build (F-013). **(1) The seam:** the dispatcher, on every `seazn:ready`, lists CEF's children into the zygote watch (`watch_ready`) before the ready is logged or sent: a ready means a renderer runs, so both zygotes exist, and nothing that hears of the ready (the domain, and the probe, which acts on `cef ready gen=1`) can act before the watch has its baseline. The child poll is unchanged; the two share `read_zygotes`. **The cost (the PR #51 review's G1, unmeasured):** each ready now walks `/proc` once on the dispatcher (every pid's `stat`, each descendant's `cmdline`); a `cmdline` read takes the child's `mmap_lock`, so a CEF child holding it (a large renderer's exit, memory pressure) stalls the dispatcher, the one thread that relays `CefAlive`, `CefCrashed` and the page's lines; about 15 to 20 processes in the container, and the poll already pays it every second. C15's soak measures it. **(2) The GPU-death count stays the poll's alone:** a list applied after a newer one can put a dead GPU process back among the live and count its death twice. **(3) "Fewer than 2 zygotes at the ready" is no verdict (the implementer's choice, medium):** two is F-013's measurement of CEF 139, not a contract, so a CEF with another count would restart every session at its first ready (and web ends a session at its 7th restart, Owed 72); and a zygote never listed has no pid for `alive` to confirm gone, so one failed command-line read would read as a death, which the watch's own rule refuses. What is left is Owed 70 (e), narrowed: a zygote that dies between CEF's start and the first ready. **(4) The probe** (`scripts/image_probe.py`): the zygote kill waits 2 × `CHILD_POLL_MS` (read from `cef_graphics.rs` through `lib_const`'s new path argument) after the ready, re-reads the process table and requires the pid to still be a zygote child of the compositor, and requires `kill` to return 0; the 2nd and 3rd zygote kills of each 4 come at once after the ready (exits 14 and 22 of 32, one at each profile), which since this fix is no race. They are not the fix's regression witness: if the fix were reverted, the probe's kills would go red only about 1 in 44. The reliable witness is the image test with the poll held, `a_zygote_killed_before_any_poll_listed_it_is_reported`. The others wait (the restart alone; the PR #51 review's m3); `wait_exit` reports `running`, never 0, for a container that outlived its wait (`docker inspect`'s ExitCode of a running container is 0: the failing exit read "exit status 0 (30.2 s)"). About 4 s more per run. **Tests:** `a_zygote_killed_between_the_ready_and_the_next_poll_is_the_restart_cause` (unit, on a fake process tree that `Shared` now holds: either zygote killed between the ready and the old 2nd poll is the verdict and `restart_cause` is `ZygoteGone`; the control; a second ready; an empty listing; the listing comes before the event), red before the fix (`unrecoverable` None, want `Some(1)`); `a_zygote_death_a_ready_lists_first_is_decided_and_logged_by_the_ready` (the review's m1: a poll lists, a zygote dies, gen 2's ready decides: `Some(2)`, one ERROR line logged before the ready is sent, no second line or stamp at the next poll); the image test `a_zygote_killed_before_any_poll_listed_it_is_reported` (the poll held from the first restart by the new `hold_polls` test hook, the GPU zygote killed at the ready, the verdict once the poll is let go). 3 mutants red: the ready's listing removed (at the verdict), moved after the send (at the order), and the ready's `log_zygote_gone` call removed (m1's test, at the line count) | — (C11b; Owed 56, 70) |

| 2026-10-24 | **Owed 85, review round 1 (PR #54 at `34632fd`: needs fixes; image run 40058946304 red): `-displayfd` alone, the leftover removal dropped (I1); the probe checks what `-displayfd` guarantees (C1) and makes every restart (I2); the probe last and always run (I3)** (the coordinator's message, 2026-10-24, relaying the PR #54 review). **I1 (coordinator-medium, decided here for the coordinator to confirm):** keep `-displayfd`, drop the removal of the display's lock and socket. `-displayfd` sets Xvfb's `nolock` (xorg-server 21.1.12 os/utils.c's `ProcessCommandLine`; `LockServer` returns at once), so no lock is read or written and none is left for a later life; Xvfb's transport unlinks a stale socket path before it binds (xtrans's `SocketUNIXCreateListener`; image run 40057826178's container 1: `socket=yes`, CEF ready). So beside `-displayfd` the removal had no observable effect (the stub test's mutant that puts it back changes no outcome, only what each case's Xvfb met); alone it would keep the socket-file wait that a leftover socket fools. The kept half's real-Xvfb test: the probe's per-restart "one Xvfb with -displayfd" and "no X lock" (deterministic on a revert) and "CEF ready again" (C14: 13 of 18 failed); the revert itself, the pre-fix start on this head, runs in throwaway draft PR #56 (image run 40078033434, to be read and the PR closed). Not observable on the real Xvfb: `-displayfd` kept with the socket-file wait back (Xvfb binds before CEF connects in a normal start); the stub's die, refuse and wrong-display cases kill it. **C1:** after each restart, exactly one Xvfb with `-displayfd`, no `/tmp/.X99-lock`, and a listening `/tmp/.X11-unix/X99` entry in /proc/net/unix whose inode that Xvfb holds; per container the witness shows one start per life, no start that met a lock, and each restart meeting the previous life's socket (the first start none). **I2:** a restart is gated only on what it needs (`up = state == "ready" and running()`); a zygote kind with no CEF SIGKILLs the compositor instead, a failure of its kind; exactly 6 checked. **I3:** the probe moves last with `!cancelled()` (one guard, rather than six followers that would also run after a failed build); `cef_cache_key_test.py` checks that a red probe hides no later step and no red check step before it hides the probe. **Minors:** m1 F-018, F-025 and the false-premise row say cited, not measured (Fly's docs, `persist_rootfs` default `never`, web sets none; measured only across 10 starts after a requested stop); m2 the step's comment (13 s and 18 s measured, timeout 15 min) and the kept log written per container; m3 `Xvfb ready after N ms` logged by `start_xvfb` and printed by the probe; m4 folded into I1 (the witness now records the socket each restart met, and no lock); m5 Owed 85 "in review" until a green image run; m6 a line naming another display reported as such. **Mutants:** xvfb.sh 12 against the stub (10 red on outcomes; M10, the removal restored, red only on the cases' precondition; M11, the duration's unit, bash 5 only, unrun locally); the probe's 12 new guards against a fake `docker` (12 red); image.yml's guard 2 (2 red) | — |
| 2026-10-24 | **Owed 85 (PR #54, branch `fix/owed85-cef-restart`; in review): the entrypoint starts Xvfb with `-displayfd` and waits for its own ready line** (the coordinator's dispatch, 2026-10-24; the cause proven first, F-025; corrected by review round 1, the row above). **Cause:** a restart that keeps the container's writable layer (compose's, Docker's `--restart`) leaves `/tmp/.X99-lock` and `/tmp/.X11-unix/X99`; the new pid namespace gives the new Xvfb its predecessor's pid (10); Xvfb's lock check (`kill(pid, 0)`) finds that pid live, itself, and exits ("Server is already active for display 99"); the old socket file passed the entrypoint's `[ -e ]` wait, so CEF found no X server (image run 40057826178: 2 of the 4 restarts made; C14: 13 of 18, twice). **Fix:** `docker/xvfb.sh`, sourced by the entrypoint (`start_xvfb 99 /tmp`): Xvfb started with `-displayfd`, which sets its `nolock`, so no lock is read or written; ready only on its own display line, in a file new at each start; an Xvfb that exits first fails the start at once (70). As first built (`e7ab150`) it also removed the display's lock and socket first, and this row called that what made a restart safe; review round 1 dropped it. **Tests:** `scripts/xvfb_start_test.py` (check.yml; a stub Xvfb; 12 cases since review round 1, whose stub follows the real binary: the first stub refused a lock even under `-displayfd`, so its 9 mutants were measured against a binary that does not exist); image.yml's in-place restart probe (`image_probe.py --restarts 6`; the real Xvfb under Docker's `--restart on-failure:2`; 75, 133 and 137 each twice; red on the unchanged entrypoint, run 40057826178). **Ruled out:** Chromium's singleton files and `/dev/shm` leftovers (`/dev/shm` is fresh at each start). **Fly:** a reset rootfs (cited from Fly's docs, not measured across an `on-failure` restart: F-018's correction) leaves nothing to meet; the fix is a no-op there | — |

## False premises found

| Premise | Reality | Found |
|---|---|---|
| CF SRT playback is single-reader; a second reader kicks the first (spec §3.1–3.2; C14 case 3 `reader-kicked`; `inputKickAlert`) | No kick from the same or a different IP (F-002). A closed playback is still possible (CF maintenance), so the reconnect path and case 3 stay; the alert's wording and the runbook must not claim "a second reader" as the cause | C00 F-002 |
| The C00/C12 Dockerfile deps are enough to link gstcefsrc | Also needs `libcups2t64`, `libpango-1.0-0`, `libcairo2` (C12's image) | C00 F-001 |
| `cefsrc ! video/x-raw,framerate=30/1` sets the rate | Caps don't negotiate; use cefsrc's `max-video-framerate` (C10/C11) | C00 F-001 |
| CEF alpha is straight | Premultiplied: C11 must unpremultiply (or composite as premultiplied) | C00 F-001 |
| Readiness via js-signals | `listen-for-js-signals=false` + page `console.info("seazn:ready")` read from the `cefconsole` category. The js-signal route hangs `start()` and `set_state(NULL)` when the page never signals (C11, R2005's page) | C00 F-001 |
| YouTube health reflects our send rate | Static, filler-padded content went Poor ~1 min in at 2.5/4/6 Mbps with a steady send; 0 %-filler snow stayed Excellent (confounded by keyframe density). Slate must carry real picture (C09); decided with real footage | C00 F-004 |
| `R2-compositor.md` in `seazn.club` describes the R2 to build | It describes B3, which the bench showed fails (156 dup / 84 gap) and which was struck in Sept. B2 replaced it, and ADR 0001 now supersedes B2. | 2026-10-14 |
| The capture app's `deliveredLagS` gives the camera delay | It is relative lag growth on the HLS manifest, not absolute delay. | 2026-10-14 |
| `composed` is unreachable because the organiser UI only posts `passthrough` | The API accepts `mode: "composed"`, and `runner-fly.ts` then throws because `RELAY_IMAGE` is unset. This is a live hazard (spec §8 item 1). | 2026-10-14 |
| "Build every adapter before the loop": `GstInput::new(frames, tx, &t, w, h)` at start (C12 Step 5; the Interfaces) | Its frame size is the session's output profile, unknown before the fetch. C12 builds it lazily at the first rebuild after the programme starts (`LazyInput`) | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "fake-control serves `reply-ending.json`…" (C12's final-heartbeat section and amendments): a harness to drive | No fake-control exists (C13, the harness, is not built). C12's `tests/fake_run.rs` hosts its own tiny_http server, and fake mode's `COMPOSITOR_FAKE_CONTROL=http` points the real plane at it | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "Move `tiny_http = "0.12"` from C07's `[dev-dependencies]`" (C12 amendment 2026-10-15, Files) | C07 never had it: its tests used a raw `TcpListener`. C12 adds it to the adapters' dependencies | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| The brief's Dockerfile builds the image (`ARG GSTCEF_COMMIT   # from F-001`; its two apt lists) | A trailing `#` on an instruction line is part of the instruction, not a comment, and both lists are short of F-001's and the spike's (libcups2t64, libpango, libcairo, the X and atk set). C12 follows F-001 and keeps the pin in `docker/GSTCEF_COMMIT` | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| `R1_STOP_GRACE_MS` "in `crates/compositor/src/main.rs`" (C12, Signals) | A test cannot import from a binary target; it is in `crates/compositor/src/lib.rs` | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "Exit 75 … its last POST `fatal`" (C12 Acceptance, amended 2026-10-20) | Since web's #1000 (`dec7b95d9`, Owed 66) the word is `restart`; the acceptance line is amended | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "exec the compositor so it receives SIGINT directly from tini" (C12's entrypoint sketch) | Once CEF starts in the process, a SIGINT or SIGTERM the compositor receives exits it 0 at once, with no Final scene, drain or final beat (F-016). The entrypoint forwards them as SIGUSR1 and SIGUSR2 | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "`forbid(unsafe)` rules out `remove_var`" (the PR #36 review's m2) | The workspace is edition 2021, where `std::env::remove_var` is a safe function; `main` removes `JOB_TOKEN` after the parse, before any thread exists | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "RLIMIT_CORE 0 … the limit is ours either way" (Owed 68's closure; C12's amendment of 2026-10-20) | For a piped `core_pattern` the kernel ignores the limit (F-018): the CI runner's crash helper got a core of a compositor whose limit read 0 0. Fly's pattern is a file, where 0 holds (measured) | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "a restart that keeps the rootfs (`restart: on-failure`) keeps it readable" (Owed 58) | On a Fly Machine the rootfs is reset at every start, an `on-failure` restart included, and `/dev/shm` is a fresh tmpfs (measured, Machine `48e4533c564ed8`) | C12, 2026-10-20 |
| "`fly machine stop` sends SIGINT by default" (flyctl's help) | It sends the Machine's `stop_config` signal: SIGTERM for web's Machines (measured). Web names SIGINT itself | C12, 2026-10-20 |

| An existing per-fixture `overlay_delay_ms` can be overridden | The column is on `fixture_stream_sessions` with default 0, and no code path or API ever writes it. Every session reads 0. | 2026-10-14 |
| The hazard throw is harmless because nothing gets provisioned | The throw happens after the session row, the CF input and provisioning already exist. R2001 must refuse in `admit()` before any write. | 2026-10-14 |
| R1 has a "degraded" heartbeat state to reuse | There is no degraded concept. Staleness is one `STALE_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS = 90` check in `expiry.ts`. | 2026-10-14 |
| A domain unit test can print its count with `eprintln!` (C03 `unlisted_pairs_hold_their_phase`) | The crate root's `#![forbid(clippy::print_stderr)]` (C01) covers inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules too: clippy `--all-targets -D warnings` refuses it (run in C02). A sweep that must print goes in `crates/domain/tests/` (C02's `foundations.rs`, C06's `properties.rs`); an inline one carries its count in the assertion. | C02, 2026-10-15 |
| Acked RTMP bytes show the output is alive (C04: "5 s with no acked bytes" is a stall; C09: "the first acked bytes → `OutputConnected{gen}`", acked bytes feed `bitrate_kbps` / `egress_bytes`) | F-005: CF Input B sent no RTMP acknowledgements; rtmp2sink `out-bytes-acked` stayed 0 in its 290 s pushes while `out-bytes-total` grew. On the live sink the ack stall would fire on every healthy push and `OutputConnected` would never arrive. C04 and C09 need a signal the live sink actually produces (for example `out-bytes-total` progress), proven on a real Input B, not on a `file://` target. **Resolved in the domain by C04:** `OutputLink::on_progress` reads the bytes-sent total and no longer knows acks (decision log, C04 row). **C09 still owes** feeding `out-bytes-total` and proving it grows on a healthy push and stops on a stalled one, on a real Input B (Owed 13). | C02, 2026-10-15; resolved in C04 |
| One output recovery fits the 15 s broadcast split (F-004 addendum), however many attempts it takes (C04 retry loop, C14 budgets) | It fits ONE attempt. Stall detected at 5 s (`output_ack_stall_ms`), back-off 0.5 s, a connect that times out at 5 s (`output_connect_timeout_ms`; F-004 rerun 1 saw exactly that, "Socket I/O timed out"), back-off 1 s: attempt 2 starts at 11.5 s with 3.5 s left. C02 pins only the single-attempt path (5 + 2 + 5 = 12 s). C04/C14 must budget the second attempt explicitly, or shorten a timeout. **C04 budgeted it through the real `OutputLink`:** the next row. | C02, 2026-10-15 |
| Reconnecting with back-off keeps every output recovery under F-004's 15 s split (C04 brief: "backs off from 0.5 s up to 2 s so a reconnect lands inside 5 s") | Only some paths fit. Worst cases through the real `OutputLink` on a 1 ms grid, where each failed connect hangs to the 5 s `output_connect_timeout_ms` and the connect that succeeds sends its first bytes 1 ms before its own timeout, are pinned to formulas from the thresholds by `tests/links.rs` `output_recovery_paths_against_the_f004_budget_on_a_1_ms_grid`. **From an error:** 5 499 ms with no failed connect, **11 499 ms through one** (fits; F-004 rerun 1 saw one), 18 499 ms through two. **From the last bytes:** after a stall, 9 999 ms, then 15 499 ms through one failed connect; after an error that lands 4 999 ms into a silence (just before the stall rule), 10 498 ms, then 16 498 ms. On the 30 fps tick grid each timed step waits for the next tick: the worst from an error through one failed connect was 11.531 s (`the_budget_holds_on_the_frame_tick_grid_at_every_phase`, 272 cases, both modes). A realistic successful connect shortens the last term: if its first bytes leave c after the build, two failed connects from an error fit only for c < 1.5 s, one failed connect after a stall only for c < 4.5 s, and after a late error only for c < 3.5 s. C09's 1 s byte poll moves both ends: the true last byte can be up to one poll before the last growth the domain saw, and first bytes reach the domain up to one poll late, so a connect whose first bytes leave after 4 s can be timed out while it works. F-004's 15 s is itself a safe floor, not the split point: it was timed kill → restart, so the no-data gap was longer (Owed 14). | C04, 2026-10-15 |
| The C03 brief's inline tests can collect phases in a `HashSet` (`table_has_no_duplicate_keys`, `every_phase_is_reachable_from_waiting`) | `purity.rs` tokenises every file under `domain/src`, inline `#[cfg(test)]` modules included, and refuses the bare name `HashSet` (probe: one `use std::collections::HashSet;` in `programme.rs`'s test module turned `domain_source_has_no_clock_env_fs_net_thread_or_process` red). C03 compares the rows pairwise and collects phases in a `Vec`; any later domain task's inline test uses `BTreeSet` or a `Vec`. | C03, 2026-10-15 |
| "1 s of continuous frames" is the time since the run's first frame (C03 brief Step 3: `Continuous` when `elapsed(now, run_since) >= resume_after_ms`) | That counts silence as frames: a camera that returns for 0.6 s and stops again reads Continuous 1 s after its return (the newest frame is only 0.4 s old), so Reconnecting goes live on a stale frame and freezes 0.1 s later. C03 measures the run as the span from its first frame to its latest, which needs frames that actually cover 1 s (tests `a_short_return_then_silence_is_not_a_second_of_frames`, `a_tick_one_second_after_the_first_frame_is_not_a_second_of_frames`; the brief's form survives the brief's own 13 tests). C06's P2 oracle (`now - run_start >= resume_after_ms`) still holds, being weaker, and C06's mutant 3 (`resume_after_ms` → `frozen_after_ms` in `feed`) still applies. **The cost (C03 review):** Live comes back at most `resume_after_ms` + the largest gap inside the returned run + one tick after the first returned frame, so the 1.2 s budget holds for in-run gaps up to 1 200 − 1 000 − 34 = 166 ms. F-002's measured maximum inter-frame gaps fit it: 69.55 ms (120 s burst), 71.5 ms (second reader) and 108.8 ms (the CPU-bound source). The owner's phone session (F-002 Step B) saw one 328 ms gap in 4 min, though, and a gap that size on the 1 s mark of a return makes Live up to ≈ 1.36 s. Pinned by `the_pdf_budgets_hold_in_both_modes_at_every_grid_phase`'s gap rows. 110 ms holds the budget (worst 1 134 ms after the return). 200 ms and 300 ms hold the bound, and both miss the budget: 200 ms in 64 of 2 448 slated cases (worst 1 201 ms), 300 ms in 268 (worst 1 301 ms). | C03, 2026-10-15 |
| Audio follows the frame choice: "live audio, or silence generated on the programme clock" (spec §3.3) | F-004: digital-silence AAC is what makes YouTube report Poor. `FrameChoice` picks the video layer only and carries no audio decision; the audio track runs on in every programme phase with no PTS gap, real input audio or low-level noise (C09's Audio section). Nothing downstream (C06, C08, C09) may map `Slate`/`Repeat` to silence. | C03, 2026-10-15 |
| `now` is a monotonic clock (`time.rs`: "milliseconds on a monotonic clock that the app layer supplies") | The C12 brief as first written set `now_ms` = `SystemTime::now()` since `UNIX_EPOCH` on every call, the wall clock, and C08's `run` hands that `now` to every `decide`. An NTP step backwards (or a VM resume) by D ms would make every age read 0, because `elapsed` saturates. A Reconnecting programme whose last run spanned ≥ 1 s would then read Continuous and go Live on its old frame with no new frame. A real loss would be detected D ms late, and frames stamped before the latest are ignored (C03) until the clock passes it again. A step forwards would age the latest frame at once (a spurious freeze or slate) and fire back-offs and the input stall rule early. Event stamps are event-loop time, so an SRT rebuild cannot reset them, and C03's ignore-earlier-stamps guard is safe on a monotonic `now`. **Fixed in the C12 brief (#14):** `now_ms` = `Instant::now()` minus an `Instant` captured at process start, plus one epoch offset (`SystemTime` since `UNIX_EPOCH`) captured once at that same moment. Ages, thresholds and back-offs run on a clock no wall-clock step can move back, and the fixed offset keeps `now` comparable with the epoch `deadline_ms`. Pinned there by `now_ms_is_monotonic_across_a_wall_clock_step` (mutant: read `SystemTime` per call). | C03 review, 2026-10-15 |
| The organiser's end reaches the compositor as `Desired::Ending` first, so the final hold can hang off that reply (overlay spec §6, C06 row) | R1's runner moves to `stopping` and sends `SIGINT` immediately, on the organiser's stop and on the deadline (`seazn.club` `apps/web/src/server/relay/domain/runner.ts`, path corrected by the review: `:101`, `:117-118`, `:251-252` on `b01d07cd5`; `:143`, `:160-161`, `:324-325` after R2003 #967). Fly SIGKILLs `RUNNER_STOP_GRACE_SECONDS = 10` later (`config.ts:141`, `:147`; `:146`, `:161` after #967). The reply comes up to a beat later, or never. C06 holds on the first of reply/SIGINT/deadline. And hold 10 s + drain 7 s = 17 s > 10 s grace: resolved by the owner's 20 s ruling (D15, 2026-10-16). | overlay amendment, 2026-10-15 |
| SIGTERM is the platform's stop, so C06 can tell a host stop from ours by the signal (C06 amendment as first written) | Fly's default stop signal is **SIGINT** (`kill_signal`), with a 5 s `kill_timeout`. Recorded in the C11, C13 and C14 amendments | Owed 18 closure, 2026-10-18 |

| A silent Ready page stays Ready until its recycle or a crash (C05b brief, Interfaces: "C06's matches use `Ready { .. }`, so they are unaffected") | True of C06's matches, but not of the tests. Under C05b a Ready page that does not beat hangs `cef_hang_after_ms` after its ready. Seven C05 inline tests (`scheduled_recycle_is_a_restart_not_a_failure`, `stale_generation_crash_is_ignored`, `a_ready_page_is_recycled_at_exactly_the_recycle_interval`, `a_recycle_records_no_failure_even_beside_fresh_failures`, `a_ready_from_another_generation_does_not_make_the_page_ready`, `a_second_ready_does_not_postpone_the_recycle`, `the_machine_reads_the_cef_thresholds`) took a silent page to its 2 h recycle, and the matrix's `(Ready, Tick)` row was the recycle. The brief named only the integration sweeps. Each of those tests now beats its page once, 1 ms before the recycle (the domain keeps only the last beat), and the matrix has a Ready setup per beat shape. Downstream, two checks hold only while the new page beats: C06's `a_ready_page_that_stops_beating_restarts` ("Then `CefReady{2}` … no further `HideOverlay`") and C08's positive pair ("`CefReady{n+1}` … No `HideOverlay` follows"). Both are amended inline (2026-10-18) to beat that generation or to stay within `cef_hang_after_ms` of its ready | C05b, 2026-10-18 |

A dedicated `graphics hung gen=<n>` line would need a hang-specific effect from C06, which the design rules out, and it is not needed. | The final-beat test's triggers: "404 → SessionGone" ("the session GET or heartbeat returned 404") and "`ProgrammeStartFailed` → Fatal" (C06 brief, final-beat section) | `SessionGone` is a 410 at bootstrap (`Refusal::Gone`; the brief's own refusal test and C07's status table); a 404 is `Refused`, exit 78. Worst cases through the real `OutputLink` (1 ms grid; each failed connect hangs to the 5 s timeout, the good one sends 1 ms before its own), for 0 / 1 / 2 failed connects: **from an error** 5 499 / 11 499 / 18 499 ms; **after a stall**, from the last bytes, 9 999 / 15 499 / 21 499 ms; **after an error 4 999 ms into a silence**, from the last bytes, 10 498 / 16 498 / 23 498 ms (the C04 reviewer derived the same table independently). | 17 | **The hide must hold against the hidden page's own frames, and lift for the retried page** (false-premise row "`HideOverlay` keeps a stale score off air"). Both directions are C05's contract. (i) After `HideOverlay`, nothing of the hidden generation reaches the programme until the next `RestartGraphics`; today's briefs do not keep that for a page that never signalled ready. (ii) Once the retried generation signals ready after the cooldown, its frames DO reach air. A sticky hide flag in C09 is a defect: it is a valid reading of C09's "a transparent frame if the overlay is hidden", C08 maps `RestartGraphics` to `graphics.restart` alone, and no `show_overlay` port exists, so nothing would ever lift it and C05's recoverable Failed would be an inert seam on air. **Coordinator decision (2026-10-16), closing it:** C11 never sets the overlay slot from a generation until that generation's own `seazn:ready` line (until then the previous generation's last frame stays held, or nothing on the first), and C08 maps `HideOverlay` to `programme.hide_overlay()` **and** `graphics.stop()`, which kills the hidden page so no late ready can re-open it, and frees its CPU for the cooldown. Both are tested with the positive pair: under `?noready=1` for `cef_fail_count` load timeouts, no overlay pixel reaches the programme from the hide through the cooldown; then a healthy page after `retry_at` signals ready and its overlay pixel IS present. This also closes the earlier open question: a never-ready page's frames no longer reach air before its ready, opaque or not. The ~33 s before the hide (3 × 10 s load timeouts plus the 1 s and 2 s back-offs) is arithmetic, pinned by C05's tick-grid sweep; whether CEF 139 paints a 404, an edge 52x or a Chromium net-error page opaque is unmeasured, and C11 measures it. | 26 | **Composed sessions get no destination-output health on web.** Web reads the CF output's state (spec §5.6 D3) only while it polls a passthrough session's input. A composed session's output lives on Input B, and nothing reads it, so the organiser's panel shows no output health for a composed broadcast. The compositor cannot see that leg either (it publishes to B, never to the destination). **Money consequence:** if a composed broadcast's destination output fails mid-stream, the organiser is still charged for the credit and sees no warning. Only the start-time case is closed: an output that is never added fails the start, consuming no credit (PR #981 review I3). Found building R2004 and left out of its scope | Nothing in R2; any "is YouTube receiving it" signal on a composed stream | web (a follow-up after R2004; R2006 shows it) |

The compositor exits 75 (`ExitReason::Restart`) after a final beat it sends as `exitReason: "fatal"` with `exitDetail` `restart in place: <cause>`, because web's beat enum is strict (`BEAT_EXIT_REASONS`, `apps/web/src/server/relay/domain/runner.ts`; the zod enum in `apps/web/src/server/api-v1/schemas.ts`) and `fatal` is the one word whose path web runs as an in-place restart. RUNNER_CRASH_CAP (3) then fails the session `machine_crash` at the third restart-or-crash. **The web change (seazn.club, not made here):** (a) a word (for example `restart`) in R2002's `heartbeat.exitReason` and `BEAT_EXIT_REASONS`, and the contract's `exitReason` description (`contracts/relay-job.v1.json:453`, "fatal exits 70") gains "restart exits 75" (today the interim breaks that sentence: `fatal` with exit 75; no web code reads the 70); (b) `stoppedBeatTrigger` maps the word to record-only (Fly restarts it in place; the composed stale window covers one that does not come back); (c) `crashExitSeen` exempts exit 75 as it exempts 78, BEFORE the `restarting`/`exitRestarted` check; (d) the `restart_count` floor counts crash restarts only (subtract the attempt's restart exits or restart beats seen in the window, or drop the floor for an attempt that has seen one); (e) a web test: restart, restart, crash in one Fly window counts 1 crash, and the session lives. Without (d), two restarts and one crash in one window reach the cap after ONE crash. **A cap of its own** is web's and the owner's call, with this case for it: once restarts are not counted, a slow loop (one restart every 165 s or more never exhausts Fly's 2 per ~330 s window, F-007) has no cap, a ~15 s outage every few minutes for the rest of the session. **Then the compositor:** re-vendor the contract, map `Restart` to the word in `exit_reason_wire`, and move the contract sweep's `restart` row (marked as not from the contract) and `the_final_beat_under_a_410_is_gone` with it. **It lands before the first live session** (C12 is the first binary that can exit 75: `crates/compositor/src/main.rs` is `fn main() {}` today) | — | — |

A measurement that removes the limit would let `cef_gpu_deaths_to_restart` go. (e) A zygote that dies ~~before the first poll has seen it (within about 1 s of CEF's start)~~ before the first listing that sees it, now the first ready's (amended 2026-10-23, decision log "Zygote watch at ready", F-022: the about 0.3 s between the ready and the old 2nd poll is closed; CEF's start to the first ready is not) gives no verdict: `zygote_check` decides only on a zygote it has `seen` (the C11 final review's N1 residual, now the only such path). The probe's control proves the image user reads such a file, and CEF's renderer runs unsandboxed as that user (`_FINDINGS.md` F-001: no-sandbox), so a compromised renderer reads the token from any of the three, whatever its own environment holds: "CEF's children never inherit it" (`main.rs`) is true, and buys less than it reads. Reaching it needs a renderer compromise (the page is web's own overlay page). **Recommendation (not built; token passing is out of this PR):** no dumpable process ever holds the token. A small launcher as the image's entrypoint moves `JOB_TOKEN` from its environment into a pipe and execs tini with the variable removed (an `execve` replaces the environment file); the entrypoint and the waiter hand the pipe to the compositor alone, and the compositor drains it before CEF starts. The alternative is Chromium's renderer sandbox (`GST_CEF_SANDBOX`), which this image leaves off. | 85 | **CEF never starts again after a container restart, so the overlay stays off** **IN REVIEW 2026-10-24 (PR #54; image run 40058946304 red on the probe's own lock check, review round 1 since; closes when it merges on a green image run, whose id goes here)** (C14 fix round 2; F-024, F-025). **Measured (C14):** after 13 of 18 container restarts in chaos runs 40051995233 and 40051902711, and 13 of 18 again in round 2b (40056474269, 40056388445), the restarted compositor's first build failed `init_cef` about 150 ms after the container's start, and graphics went `failed` for the rest of the run, after every exit kind (75, 133, 137); a first start never failed. **Cause (proven, F-025):** the restart keeps `/tmp`; the new Xvfb gets its predecessor's pid (10), so its lock check (`kill(pid, 0)`) finds a live process, itself, and it exits "Server is already active for display 99" (the real Xvfb's stderr, image run 40057826178); the old socket file passed the entrypoint's `[ -e ]` wait, and CEF found no server. A recovery is a life whose first pids shifted by one. Not Chromium's singleton files, not `/dev/shm` (fresh at each start). **Fix:** `docker/xvfb.sh` starts Xvfb with `-displayfd`, which sets its `nolock` (no lock read or written), and waits for Xvfb's own line on that fd; an Xvfb that exits first fails the start (70); an earlier life's socket Xvfb replaces itself (decision log "Owed 85, review round 1": the first build's removal of leftovers dropped). **Witness:** image.yml's in-place restart probe (6 restarts, each kind twice, every one made; CEF ready, one Xvfb with `-displayfd`, no lock, its socket listening; Docker's `--restart on-failure:2`) and check.yml's `xvfb_start_test.py`; the `-displayfd` revert on the real Xvfb: throwaway PR #56, image run 40078033434. **Fly:** its rootfs is reset at each start (cited from Fly's docs, not measured across an `on-failure` restart; false-premise row, Owed 85), so nothing is left there to meet. **Still the C14 dispatch's (not this PR):** the recommendation to add case 8's `cef_ready_after_restart` to `require_in_place_restart` (cases 13–15) | C14 cases 8, 9 and 13–15 (their restart reds, until this merges) | — |
